# Paul of Aleppo II (Paul d'Alep, archidiacre d'Antioche, II)

**Author**: Paul d'Alep, archidiacre d'Antioche  
**Series**: PO Tome 24  
**Columns**: pp. 442–605  

[p. 442] {#po.24.p442}
طقس صيام الكبير في البغضان!
XIV. - Rite of the Great Lent in Moldavia.
نعاود؟ ان يوم سبت مرفع الجبن العاده انهم بيطلقوا جميع من في الحبوس لان فى الجمعه الاولي من الصوم بترتفع المحاكمات والمماحكات والبيك ما بيظبر لاحداً اصلًا لا في الكنيسه مواضب صيامه هو وجميع اكابرة وغلمانه وعشية احد المرفع المدكور عملوا بعد الان اطلق عبدك4 ثلاث مطانيات كبار لاغير وختموا الصلاه ثم بولبخرونيون البيك وما لهم عاد يقولوا صلاة النوم الكبيرة في الكنايس حتي ولا عشية حدود الصيام كله ولكن في قلالييم ثم ان روسا الكبنة الحاضرين والكبنة وجميع من كان حاض في الكنيسه عملوا لسيدنا البطرك مطانيات اثنين اثنين الي آخرهم اصبحنا اثنين النقى وخرجنا تفرحنا على المحتسب الداير ومعه الفلق والعصى لقتل الذي خمارته مفتوحه وللني بيقط ثم بيرق جميع المواكيل بالريض
Let us return to our subject. On the Saturday of abstinence from cheese, the custom is to set all the prisoners free, because the first week of Lent the courts are closed and trials are suspended. One does not see the prince at all except at church, faithful to the fast, he, all his boyars, as well as his pages. On the Sunday of the aforementioned abstinence, after the prayer: "Lord, now let your servant depart," they made three great genuflections only and finished the service. Then, they performed the "polychronion" for the prince, but they are not in the habit of performing the office of Great Compline in the churches, even on the Sunday evenings of Lent, but they do it in their cells. Then the high clergy present, the priests, and all the assistants in the church made genuflections, two by two, until the last one before my lord the patriarch. On the Monday morning of the Purification, we went out to see the police commissioner who was making his rounds, with the instrument of torture named falaq and sticks to punish those whose taverns were open and those who were distilling. It is he who fixes the price of food items.


[p. 444] {#po.24.p444}
اعلم ان جميع المواكيل في ساين هذه البلدان النساة بتبيعهم ثم انهم في الديورة عملوا ناقون خشب جديد وعد دق الساعة الثامنه قرعه القندلفت ثلاث قرعات فدخلنا للكنيسه وقلوا الساعة الثالثة وفي اخرها خرج قرعه ثلاث مرات ووقف ثم قرعه ثلاث اخري اي علامه انها الساعة السادسه وفى اخرها ابض خرج قرعه ثلاثه ووقف ثم ثلاثه اخري ووقف ثم ثلاثه اخري تمام التسعه اي على الساعة التاسعة وعند آخر الماكارذمي طلع للبرج وقرع ناقون الخشب العاده ثم ناقوس الصغير علامة لصلاة الغروب وهكذا فعلوا جميع الصوم لاخر وجميع بازركان الروم كانوا متقيدين! في حضور السواعي المدكورة الي اخر صلاة الغروب الصوم جميعه ثم خرحنا من الكنيسه وعاودنا اليها بعد ساعتين ثم ابتدوا بصلاة انوم الكبير وحين قول قانون الاقريطشي كانوا يطرحوه بترئيم فى الخورصين وقادي صلاة النوم بيقول ايتها السيدة واعطنا ايها السيد ولو انه صبى وسيدنا البطرك بيقول افشين ايها السيد الرب يسوع المسيح الاهثا كالعادة عندهم وجميعهم بيسجدوا للارض حين قراآته الي ان يفرغ بيقوموا! وعند الاخر
Remark. It is the women who sell all the foodstuffs at retail in all these countries. In the convents, they made a new simandron. At the eight o'clock strike, the sacristan struck three times. We entered the church and the third hour was recited; at the end, he went out, rang three times and stopped, then three other times, to indicate the sixth hour, then he went out, rang three times and stopped, then again three times and stopped, again three times, that is nine times, to indicate the ninth hour. At the moment of the "Beatitudes," he went up into the bell tower, struck the ordinary simandron, then the small bell to announce the vesper service. They did the same during all of Lent until the end. All the merchants showed themselves assiduous in attending the service of the hours, until the end of vespers during all of Lent. We then left the church and returned there two hours later, then they began the office of Great Compline. While they were reciting the canon of Saint Andrew of Crete, the two choirs sang it by chanting. The reader of the Compline office says: "O holy Virgin!" and "grant us, O Lord," although this reader is a child. My lord the patriarch reads the prayer: "O Master Lord Jesus Christ, our God," and as is the custom among them, everyone prostrates themselves to the ground during the recitation until it is finished, then they rise. * Then everyone comes


[p. 445] {#po.24.p445}
جميعهم! بيجوا يركعوا له اثنين اثنين حتي والاولاد ونصرفوا ثم وهو خارج كانوا يعوا النس جميعهم وهكذا كانوا يفعلوا جميع الصوم وكذلك دخنا للكنيسه يوم الثلثا وعشية هكذا صار واما يوم الارعاء فجميع الأديرة أكثر تجار الروم حتي والبيك وحرمته وجبيع من في صرايته من البيواربه الي اولاد الخزنه بيصوموا هذ الثلاث حمله وما بيفطروا العشية الاربسا المسآ بعد قداس البرونجاسمانا والخمر قطعاً ما بيدوقونا فى هذه الجمعه ولا فى كل الصوم ما خلا يوم السبت والاحد لاغير وعشية الارسا المدكور دقوا ناقون السواعى اول الساعة السابعة وكذلك كان يخرج القندلفت يدق على كل ساعة ثائه الى اخن الماكازرمي طلع للبرج دق ناقوس الخشب ثم جرص الكبير اى ناقون النحاس علامه لقداس البروبجاسمانا ثم ابتدوا به وعند 0«0660دهجه»:3 صلاها الكاهن فى البيكل اول ثم ترنموها مناقله في الخورصين اديع مرات ثم قلبا الكاهن سادننً الى نصفها وكملوها خارجً واما قاري النبوت فما عمل ولا مطانية قدام ابواب البيكل ولكن لسيدنا البطرك فقط ولما خرج الكاهن فى دورة الجسد ما وصل الى الخورص لكنه خرج من
before him, make genuflections two by two, including the children, and withdraw. Upon his exit, all the women make genuflections as he passes. They do this during all of Lent. On Tuesday we also went to the church and we did the same in the evening. On Wednesday all the convents and most of the Greek merchants and even the prince, his wife, and all the people of the palace, from the boyars to the children of the princely house, all fasted these three days, and they did not break the fast until Wednesday evening after the Mass of the Presanctified. One does not drink wine during this week nor during all of Lent, with the exception only of Saturday and Sunday. This Wednesday evening, they rang the bells for the hours, first the seventh hour: the sacristan went out to ring three strokes at each hour until the end of the "Beatitudes." He then went up to the bell tower, struck the simandron, then he rang the large bell, that is to say, the bronze bell to announce the Mass of the Presanctified. They then began the Mass and at the moment of: "let my prayer be set forth before Your face," the priest sang it first in the sanctuary, then the two choirs sang it alternately four times, then the priest resumed it for the sixth time until the middle and they finished it outside. The reader of the prophecies does not make any genuflection before the sanctuary doors, but only before my lord the patriarch. When the


[p. 446] {#po.24.p446}
الباب الشمالى ودخل فيما بين الشماعدين والابقونات الى باب الملوكى وعند الكينويكن! نزل سيدا البطرك تبارك من ابقوئة مار يوحنا المعمدان التي وضعوها علي قرايا لأجل موسم عيدد في الفد وبارك عليهم حنب العادة كلما نزل تبارك من الاقونات ثم انوا جميعهم اخدوا منه انديدارا وتاركوا من الايقونه لان هذه عادتهم في كل ليلة الصوم كانوا يناولوهم انديدارا ولو ما صار قداس برويجاسمانا والكاهن بيحفظيم من قداس الاحد وبيفرتهم عليهم ثم وقفوا اهي ان خرج سيدنا الطرك قدامهم حسب العادة وخرجوا اثنين اثنين حسب عادتهم وهو يرفع يده وببارك عليهم الي ان خرجوا النسآ وهم واقفين الي ان بارك علي حميمم تان وانصرفوا وهكذا كان يصير هذا الطقس الذي ذكرا في كل الصيام واما فى بلادنا ما بيتصبروا الى ان يتح الستر وفى هذه الجمعة4 كانوا ابازركان في كل ليلة ياتا للكنيسه في حين الصلاد وبخرج الكاهن بالبطرشيل يقرا عليهم افاشين استغفار ونصنوا له لان كنيسة دير مار سابا الله يعمرها ما يصلى فيا" الا
priest went out for the procession of the holy Body, he did not reach the choir but went out through the north door and headed between the candlesticks and the icons toward the royal door. At the "Kinonikon," my lord the patriarch went down, kissed the image of Saint John the Baptist that had been placed on a "proskynetarion" on the occasion of his feast which fell the next day. He blessed them according to custom. Every time he went down, he kissed the icons. Everyone then came to receive the blessed bread from his hand and kiss the icon, for it is their custom every evening of Lent to take blessed bread, even if there had not been a Mass of the Presanctified, the priest kept some from the Sunday Mass and distributed it to them. They remained standing until my lord the patriarch passed before them according to custom and according to their usage they went out two by two. Then raising his hand, the patriarch blessed them until the moment when the women went out. They remained to be blessed a second time and they withdrew. This rite, of which we have spoken, is that which is observed during all of Lent. In our country, one does not wait until the lifting of the curtains. During this week, the merchants came every evening to the church at the time of the office; the priest went out with the stole to read them the prayer of absolution and they gave him money; for in the church of the convent of Saint Sabas—may God preserve it!—the merchants are the only ones to come to pray and among them there are many married people. As for the people of the country, they neither fast nor pray, they have no religion,


[p. 447] {#po.24.p447}
البازركان وميم كبر مزوجين واما اهل الللد فلا صيام ولا صلا ولا ديانه الا نصاري بالاسم وكبنتهم ليم بيبكروا من الليل للخمارات هذا الذي نظرئلا في بلاد اللغضان واما في بلاد الفلاخ الله يعمرهم لحسن ديانتهم وعفافهم ثم اننا جلسنا علي الطراباذا وما كان غير فول مبلول ومازر أي فاسوله مطبوخه بلا زت وهي علي شكل الجلبانه ولخنا أي ملقوف مكيون بماء وبح لطول السنه لاغين واما الخمر فكما ذكرنا انهم ما بيفطرهه وكذلك الزيت1 ما خلا السبت والاحد ولكن بيشريوا ماء تفاح واما نحن فين لنا البيك في نارات الاربعا والجمعه من الصوم وفي هذا الجمعة الاولي ما شعير وما عسل لان ما احدا فى هذ البلاد جميعها بيشرب ماء الا القليل واما الطحينة والسيرج والسسم ما بيعرفوهم قطعا حتي ان الاذركان بيجاب لبم من الربيله الزيت والزيتون والبهطارخ والاخطبوط وم الليمون والحمص والرز وشعاريه وغير ذلك واخدنا نحن اقة زيت بنصف غرش واقة زيتون بريع واقه البطارخ يقرش ونصف واما اقة الاخطبوط اليابس ترش ريال وكل اقة حمص بريع لانهم في هذا البلاد جميعهم ما بيززعوه ولا بيعرفو» الا الاغنيا
They are Christians only in name. Their priests are the first to begin their day at the tavern. This is what we saw in Moldavia. As for Wallachia, may God prolong the life of its inhabitants because of their religious sentiments and their sobriety! We then sat down to eat. There were only beans soaked in water, that is to say, peas, beans cooked without oil which resemble vetches, and cabbage * preserved in water and salt, for the whole year and nothing else. As we have said, they use neither wine nor oil, except on Saturday and Sunday, but they drink cider. As for us, the prince had us given on the Wednesdays and Fridays of Lent and during this first week, beer and mead, for in all this country no one drinks water except a few. Ground sesame, sesame oil, and sesame seeds are absolutely unknown to them, so it is the merchants who import from Turkey in Europe the oil, olives, bottarga, octopus, lemon juice, chickpeas, rice, and vermicelli, etc. We paid for an ocque of oil half a piastre, an ocque of olives a quarter of a piastre, an ocque of bottarga a piastre and a half, an ocque of dried octopus a piastre ecu, an ocque of chickpeas a quarter, because the inhabitants of all this country, with the exception of the rich, do not cultivate them and do not know them.


[p. 448] {#po.24.p448}
واما الخض مثل السلق والبقدونى والبصل الاخضر والنوم مابيطلع لبعد الفصح لان الصيام جميعه الثلج ما بينقطع ليلا ونباداً وتصبح الارض بكر مجلده مثل ابلاط والخاصه السواقي ناذا طلعت الشمسن وحمي اهار بيحل ويصير وحل وطين عظيم للركبه ولكن في الديورة وفي بيوت الاغنيا مغاير قبر حجر كيار ويسموهم بسانم إنيمجه ونيسه يوضعوا فيهم براميل الخمر وفيهم اماكن للزرع وبيقلعوا من قبل الما والثلج والبقدونس والبصل واما! الكرات فهو كثيراً جد وحلو وغير ذلك وبيزرعوه في المغاره حيث دكرنا فمن البرودة بيعيش وبيخرجوا منه متي ما ارادوا اخضر بياكلوا وبالخصه! في هذا الصيام ثم في الاديرة من بعد عشاهم في عشيه الاربسا بيشيلوا الي عشية الجيعه ايض ثم دخنا للكنيسة بعد الساعة السابعة وحين الكينونيكن3 نزل سيدنا البطرك تارك من ابقونة مار تاودورس الشاهد ومن اصبعه التي وضعوها وهي وقف علي الدير كنوك عند اخد جميعهم الانديدارا تاركوا منها
XV. - Vegetables and products. As for vegetables such as chard, parsley, white onion, and garlic, they only grow after Easter, for during all of Lent the snow does not cease to fall day and night and in the morning the earth is frozen like a slab, the streams especially are frozen. When the sun rises and the day warms up, the snow thaws and turns into a thick mud that reaches the knees. In the convents and the houses of the rich, there are large stone-vaulted cellars, called in their language pimnits or pinits where they put the barrels of wine. There, there are places to store vegetables. Before winter and the snowfall, they pull the parsley and onions from the earth to put them in the cellars. Leek is very abundant and sweet. One stores vegetables in the cellars of which we have spoken and thanks to the coolness that reigns there they grow. When one wants some, one takes some and eats them especially during Lent. In the convents after the Wednesday evening dinner, they fast until Friday evening. We then entered the church after the seventh hour and during the "Kinonikon" my lord the patriarch went down and kissed the icon of Saint Theodore the martyr, as well as a finger of this saint that they had exposed, which is a pious bequest belonging to the convent. Likewise at the distribution of the blessed bread they all kissed it.


[p. 449] {#po.24.p449}
وبعد -الساعة العاشره دقوا حرص الصغين لاغير ودخلنا للكنيسه ثم وضعوا قرايه وعليها ستر وشمع قدام ابقونة السّده وقال ربيس الدير ستة قطع من مديح سيت المدايح لاغير حسب عادتهم في ليله كل سبت من الصوم بيقولوا ستفوصاح السبت الاول خرحنا من الاورترن ثم دقوا ناقوس الكبير بعد الساعة الثلثه وما خرجوا من القداس لقرب الساعه الخامسه واحضروا في هذا اليوم صحون سليقه كثير ببسل ويارات علي اسم الشاهد تاودورس وبعد خثم القداس خرج الكاهن بخر وترنموا طرووارات القدين ثم للاموات وقال سيدنا البطرك افشين السليقه ودعآ للدين قدموها علي اسم الشاهد ولنياح موتاهم وبعد ان فرق عليهم الانديدارا وتباركوا من ايقونة الشاهد عاودوا وقفوا بكراسهم الي ان احض كل من هو صحنه الدين قدموهم قدام! سيدنا البطرك وكان يتناول منهم واحد بعد واحد بمعلقه فضه او عظم ثم يدوروا بهم علي الحاضرين الي مممامهم ثم خرج سيدنا البطرك قدامهم الي باب الكنيسة3 كالعادة " وارك عليهم وهم خارجين بالبدو اثنين اثنين! الى ان خرجوا النسا وجميعهم كانوا كالملكات مهدبات
After the tenth hour they rang only the small bell. We entered the church and they placed the lectern (tetrapodion) covered with a veil and candles before the icon of the holy Virgin. The hegumen of the convent said the six strophes of the Akathist; following their custom, every eve of the Saturday of Lent they say six. The first Saturday in the morning we came out of matins, then the large bell was rung after the third hour; one did not leave mass until the approach of the fifth hour. That day, they brought many plates of "koliva" with honey and spices in honor of Saint Theodore the martyr. After mass, the priest went out to incense; after which they sang the troparia of the saint, then they recited the prayers for the dead, and my lord the patriarch read the prayer for the "koliva" and prayed for those who had brought it in honor of the martyr, as well as for the rest of their dead. After having distributed the blessed bread to them and they had kissed the icon of the martyr, they returned to their stalls so that each of those who had offered the plates could present himself before my lord the patriarch, who took successively a little koliva with a silver or ivory spoon. One then made the rounds among the assistants until the last one. My lord the patriarch went out before them to the church door, as usual. * He blessed the faithful while they went out in silence two by two, until


[p. 450] {#po.24.p450}
محتشمات وما كانت تخرج الصبايا قدام العجايز ولكن كانوا يقدموهم دايمً قدامهم فبالطولة هذه الروح وهذا الايمان ما احدً منهم يتضج او يتدمر ومع هذا ففي هذا البرد والجليد واقفين جميعهم بلا اقباع مكشوفين الرووس وفي غاية البدو بلا حديث من اول الصلاه لاخرها ولا من صبي ولا من حريه حتي وفقراهم وهم دايرين في الكنايس بغاية البدو والسكون وكثرة المطائيات دفي نهار السبت ان كان في الصيام او في ارض السنه غير متزاحمين على اخد البروتي ولا علي تقبيل الابقونات ولا علي الخروج سريعاً ولكن اثنين اثنين لكن بلا شك ان الله بعدل وانصاف ابا ملكيم للان وفي هذا اليوم بعد القداس تاركنا ايض من حبة الشاهد ينقوب المقطع وهي مفضضه بوعاء فضه ايض وقف علي الدير المذكور مع اصبع الشاهد تاودورس ثم خرجنا للطرابازا وشريًا خسر وبعد صلاة الغروب عشيه ارسل حضرةٍ البيك وكيله السلجاري المدكور ياخد بخاطر
all the women had gone out. They were all like queens, serious and collected; the young girls did not go before the older women, but the latter always walked in front, with such patience and faith! No one was impatient, nor did anyone murmur. Despite the cold and the frost, all were standing, bareheaded, without bonnets, in perfect calm and silence, from the beginning to the end of the office: all, children or women, as well as the poor, walked sedately and in silence in the church, making many genuflections. On Saturdays, whether during Lent or during the year, they do not rush to take the blessed bread, nor to kiss the icons, nor to leave, but they go out two by two. If the days of their prince have been prolonged until this time, it is clearly because God, in His justice and equity, wished to reward him. On this day also, after the Mass, we kissed the forehead of the holy martyr James the Persian, which is covered in silver and enclosed in a reliquary of the same metal. This relic belongs to this convent, as does the finger of the holy martyr Theodore. We went out to go and sit at the table and we drank wine. XVI. - Office in the convent of Galata. In the evening, after vespers, His Highness the prince sent his representative, the "sloudjer", to greet my lord the patriarch and to ask him to allow him


[p. 451] {#po.24.p451}
سيدنا الطرك ثم باجازته يبات علي خدمة قداس في دير القلطه وارسل معه طباخه للصيام برميل زت ورميل ما ليمون وكيس اخطبوط ياس وكيس رز وكيس شعاريه وكيس عدس وكيس حمص وكيس فول وكيس مازر تحمليم الضاباتيه علي أثنانهم ثم دقوا النواقيس وقمنا سحر الاحد الاول من الصوم للكنيسة وما عملوا دورة للايقونات حبن آجيوس آو ثاوس مثل عادتنا ولكن وضعوا الابقونة علي القرايه المجلله وحين باصا بنوبي نزل سيدنا البطرك تبارك من الأيقونات نومنها وكذلك بمية الحاضرين وخرجا وعد ساعتين حضر السلجاري المدكور ومنه قوجية البيك المجلله بالجوخ الاحمن وعكر ألفضه بنت دو ليل فى ومضيئا بها مع بدلاتنا الى دين الغلطه المدكور ودخانا للكنيسه وابدانا نحن وجميع روسا الديار لان عليهم درك في اين ما مضي البيك يحض قداس بيحضروا جميعهم وبيخدموا ثم جاء خبر ان نلبس سيدنا البطرك لان حضرة البيك مايحب طول الصلاه خرحنا من البيكل وهذا صفة طقسم هاهنا ان كل ريثيس دير او كاهن خارج بيخرج وعلي يديه حاجه من بدلات سيدنا البطرك من الاستيحادي الي التاج
to spend the night at the convent of Galata to hear Mass there. At the same time, the prince sent his cook and provisions for Lent, namely: a barrel of oil, a barrel of lemonade, a sack of dried octopus, a sack of rice, a sack of vermicelli, a sack of lentils, a sack of chickpeas, a sack of fava beans, a sack of peas, all carried by soldiers on their shoulders. Then the bells were rung and on the morning of the first Sunday of Lent we went to the church. The procession of the icons was not performed during the singing of the "Holy God" as we are accustomed to doing, but the icon was placed on the veiled proskynetarion, and during "Let everything that breathes", my lord the patriarch came down to kiss the icons and especially those placed on the proskynetarion. The assistants did the same and we went out. Two hours later, the "sloudjer" arrived with the prince's carriage, trimmed with red cloth, adorned with silver braid, and drawn by six sorrel horses. In this carriage, we arrived with our vestments at this convent of Galata. Having entered the church, we put on our vestments, we and all the hegumens of the convents, for they are required to go and say Mass wherever the Prince goes to attend the holy sacrifice. Then someone came to tell us to vest my lord the patriarch, for His Highness the prince does not like the office to last too long. And we left the sanctuary. Here is the order followed for the office. Upon leaving, all the hegumens and priests carry in their hands some piece of the vestments of my lord the patriarch, from the alb


[p. 452] {#po.24.p452}
وجميعهم بيصطفوا حول وكان واقف علي يمين سيدنا البطرك كوزل افندي ريثيس دير الضومنا وعلي شماله ريشيس دير مار سابا وهم الذين كانوا يتناولوا حاجه بعد حاجه وساعدوني في اللبس وهكذا كانو يفعلوا كلما قدس سيدنا البطرك دايمً وكنا غطينا الثاج بمحريه الطون الي حين لبسه نزعناها! ثم بعد فروغنا مضي سيدنا البطرك جلس علي كرسيهة الا والنواقيس جميعها تنقرع وهي علامة لقدوم البيك وحين دخل من باب الدير خرجوا ريشيسين كبنه كانوا خادمين والشمع ثم بقية الكبنه والشمامسه بالمباخر قدام سيدنا البطرك واصطفوا خارج باب الكنيسه وكان حامل4 على شمال سيدنا البطرك كاهن بلطانيه صيني فيها ماء مقدس وربحان وكاهن اخ على يمينه بالانجيل والصليب بيد سيدنا البطرك الى ان قدم ابنه ستافانى فوضفوضا ونزل من قوجيته ووقف الى ان دخلت قاجية بيه :وكات مغطلا ببمرركش وداخليا محمل احتي. بكر قله مطلى ملوكيه افنزل منها ومشت الستة شاطرباشيه بلباسن واحد وبمحثمات وردي على رؤوسهم وفي يديهم
to the mitre, and all line up around him. To the right of my lord the patriarch stood Guzel Effendi, the hegumen of the princess's convent, and to his left, the hegumen of the convent of Saint Sabas. They received the vestments, one after the other, and they helped us to vest the patriarch. They did the same each time my lord the patriarch celebrated Mass. We had covered the mitre with a gold veil and we did not uncover it until the moment of putting it on. When my lord the patriarch had been vested in his ornaments, he sat on his throne and immediately all the bells were rung to announce the arrival of the prince. While he was entering through the gate of the convent, two of the officiating archpriests went out * with candles, as well as the other deacons and priests with censers preceding my lord the patriarch. They lined up outside the church door. To the left of my lord the patriarch stood a priest with a porcelain vase containing holy water and a sprig of basil; to his right stood another priest carrying the Gospel book. My lord the patriarch held the cross in his hand while waiting for the arrival of the prince's son, the Voivode Stephen. The latter got out of the carriage and waited standing for the entry of his father's carriage. It was covered in brocade, the interior was red velvet with gilded silver braid. He stepped out of it and six runners walked before him carrying


[p. 453] {#po.24.p453}
الاطبار المصلبه قدامه ونزلوا جميع بيواريته واكابر وخواصه عن خيوليم ومشيوا حوله واصطفت السكمان والساكر خارج الدين وداخله وكان نهار يعد من الاعمار آلا اننا نحن مسكنا! رعده وهيبه كثيرة لبيبته وحيانا كون انه اقل قداس وما عرفنا طقسه مثل عادة الملوك بعد" ثم ان البيك المدكور تمشا فلاقاد سيدنا البطرك وقبله الانجيل ثم الصليب ودش علي وجه الماء وعلي انه وباركهم اول وثانيا وبخرههم الشماسة ثم دخانا قدامه الي ان دخل ووقف امام كرسيه وصلب علي وجه ثم التفت يمينا وشملاً واحني راسه نحو الحاضرين فانحنوا له جميعهم ثم طلع وقف بكرسيه3 الذي. كانوا قد جلو ببضل الاحمر جميعه وكذلك+ فعل ابنه وبارك عليهم سيدنا البطرك ثالث ووقف بكرسيه ايض ثم دخلت اكاب الخاص؟ ووقف في الخورص الثمالي ووقف الستانيك بعساة الفضه قدام والسلحدار والتاج على راسه وهو مقلد بالسيف والدبوس على شماله وبقية خواصه فى النريكس مكشوفين الروس جميعهم لان ما احدا بيقف قدامه الا مكشوف
all the same uniform, wearing pink caps and armed with axes adorned with crosses. All the boyars, the notables, and the retinue dismounted from their horses and walked around him: the "seimens" and the soldiers formed a hedge in his path both outside and inside the convent. It was a memorable day in our life! As for us, we were seized with fear and respect for him, because it was the first Mass that we celebrated before him and we did not yet know the rules to observe with regard to sovereigns. Then the prince advanced. My lord the patriarch went to meet him, had him kiss the Gospel book and the cross, and sprinkled him with holy water, him and his son. || blessed them twice and the deacons incensed them. We entered the church before the prince; he entered in his turn and went to place himself before his throne: he made the sign of the cross, he turned to the right then to the left, bowing to the assistants who all bowed toward him. || He then mounted and stood at his throne entirely covered in red velvet. His son did the same. My lord the patriarch blessed them a third time and he also stood at his throne. The notables and the lords entered in their turn and placed themselves in the north choir. The "postelnic" with the silver staff stood before the prince, while the "silindar", wearing a crown on his head, girded with a sword and armed with a club, stood to his left. The other notables stopped in the narthex, all bareheaded, no one remaining covered before him, even at the table.


[p. 454] {#po.24.p454}
الراس ولو كان علي الطرابازا ثم ان الشماس بخ ابواب البيكل والايقونات ثم جا بخرا بكرسيه ثم لولده ولسيدنا اللبطرك ثم لخواصه ثم وقف مكانه كالعادة وقال ارحمنا يا الله كعظيم رحمتك نطلب منك فاستجيب وارحم وايضً نطاب من احل الملك المحب المسيح المحفوظ من الله الافندي ايوانو فاسيلي فوشوضا والملكه حرمته الضومانا كاترينا والملك ابنه ستافاني ويوضه ودوام السلام! له والعافيه والخلاص والرب الاهنا على الاكثر يواذد بالشوفيق كافه اموره ويخضع تحت اقدامه كل عدو ومحارب وكان الشماس دام كلما دخل البيك للكنيسه ويبخر ويقول هذه حب عادتهم دايمً واما اولاد الخزنه جميعهم بجوخ احمر كانوا يفوا في الخورصين دايمً في الخورص اليمين يصلوا باارمي وفي الشمالي بالفلاضي من فم واحد بترئيم. يجرح الفواد ونعش الخاطر فقالوا حينيذ بعد كرازة الشماس كراليصن ماقله كثيراً ثم قال الكاهن الاعلان وختم الصلاه وقالوا اولاد
The deacon then incensed the doors of the sanctuary and the icons, after which he went to incense the prince at his throne, then he incensed his son and my lord the patriarch, after which he incensed the notables. Having returned to his place, the deacon stood according to custom and said: "Have mercy on us, O God, according to Your great mercy, we beseech You, hear us and have mercy", and "we further beseech You for the Christ-loving prince, the protégé of God, the Effendi Ioanu the Voivode, Basil, for the queen his wife, the princess Catherine, and for the prince, the Voivode Stephen, his son; for them we ask You for peace, health, and salvation forever, and that the Lord our God may assist our prince in all things and favor him in all his affairs, and that He may humble under his feet every enemy and adversary." When the prince entered the church, the deacon always incensed him while reciting these same prayers, according to custom. The young pages of the princely house were all in red cloth garments and always stood in the two choirs. In the right choir, they sang in Greek and in the left one in Vlach, singing in unison and in a tone that touches the heart and rejoices the spirit. After the litany recited by the deacon, they chanted several times alternately: "Lord, have mercy"; then the priest said the conclusion and ended the office. The young pages of the princely house said the "polychronion" for the prince,


[p. 455] {#po.24.p455}
الخزنه بوليخرونيون البيك وحريته وابئه حسب العادة ثم عمل الشماس مطائيه لطيفه نحو البيك ونحو انه ونحو سيدنا البطرك لان هذا عادته دايمً قبل الكرازة وبعدها ووقف «ووجبها تحولا مشحرف قايلا الموجيصن! دسبوطا وابدا الكاهن بالقداسن ثم الشماس بالكرازة وعلي كل كبراليصن يترنموا الاولاد كيرياليصن واحد رومي واخري هناك فلاخي ثم عمل كارون نحوهم ودخل للبيكل على قفالا وهكذا كنا تفعل نحن ًا اننا كنا مرتعدين كثيرا ثم ترنموا الماكارزمي وخرجنا باليصودن خرج شماس قدام بالتريكادي كالعاده والاخر بسخا وانا بالانجيل حينيذ خرج سيدنا اللطرك من كرسيه واتا وقف تحت البوليالاون كالعاده وبارك علي الايصودن وانا مضيت حسب عادتهم الي عند البيك قلته الانجيل وقبات يمينه وكذلك لابنه ثم لسيدنا الطرك ودخانا للبيكل ولما قال سيدنا البطرك صوصون ايماص التفت تحوة وبارك عليه اول وعلي ابنه ثم علي سلحدارة ثم ولبقية خواصه ثم تبارك من الايقونات حسب العاددة ودخل بض حول المابدة وما بض
for his wife and for his son according to usage. The deacon then made a slight bow before the prince, before his son, before my lord the patriarch, before and after the litany according to his custom. Then the deacon stood, his face inclined toward the patriarch, saying: "Bless, Master". The priest began the Mass. * The deacon said the litany and at each "Lord, have mercy", the young men sang "Lord, have mercy"; on one side they sang it in Greek, on the other they sang it in Vlach; the deacon bowed toward them to greet them and he entered the sanctuary walking backward; we did the same, but we were trembling with emotion. After the singing of the "Beatitudes" we went out for the "entrance" and a deacon preceded us carrying a three-branched candlestick according to custom, another held a censer, I carried the Gospel book. My lord the patriarch then descended from his throne and, placing himself below the chandelier, he blessed the "entrance". I headed toward the prince, I had him kiss the Gospel, and I kissed his right hand as well as that of his son, then I kissed the hand of my lord the patriarch and we re-entered the sanctuary. When my lord the patriarch said: "save us", he turned toward the prince, blessed him first, then he blessed his son as well as his "silihdar" and the retinue, then he kissed the icons according to custom, and he entered to incense around the altar. || He did not incense the


[p. 456] {#po.24.p456}
فى باب الملوكى ما هى عاد ولكن عند اخر كه ايسطوس اوناس طون أونون خرج بارك عليهم بلتريكاري ثم ترنموا اولاد الخزنه باجيوس ماقله بلحن حلو جد الي ان قالوا بمم»ئة! وما قلبا سيدنا البطرك ولا الكبنة داخل اصلًا ولا الوا بولا طا اتى ايض ثم خرج الشماس الاتجيل وعمل لبم من بعيد كارون لطيف ايض وقاله تحت البوليالاون وعند فراغه مضى الى عند البيك وابنه وقليم ايلا وقل يديهم وحين بض اشاروبيكن خرج سيدا البرك بالبخرد الي" بين الشناعدين وبخرة البيك والحاشين ثم التفت بخر4 ابواب البيكل والايقونات ودخل ف(إنا خرجت بالصينيه والجند وكانوا حينيذ قد فرش فراش البيك قدام كرسيه طنفسه زركش حميعها وفي وسطبا قليل احمر مضل وكذلك قدام كرسي ابنه اخري صغير مثلها ونزلوا انطرحوا علييم للارض ولما وصات الى عندهم التفتت نحوهم كلعاده قايلا يذكر الرب الاله الملك يوانو فاسيلي فوحوضا وحربته الضومنا كاترينا وولده ستافانى فوفوضا فى ملكوته السماوية كل حين وكذلك عمل الكاهن حامل الكاس وكان سيدنا البطرك قلع تاجه ودار به احد الكبنة
royal door, for it is not the custom. At the end of: "and unto the ages of ages", he went out to bless them with the three-branched candlestick and the young men of the princely house sang alternately the "Holy God" in a very sweet melody, until they had said: "Strength"; but neither my lord the patriarch nor the priests inside said this word, nor did they say: "for many years". The deacon went out carrying the Gospel book; from afar, he made a slight inclination to greet the assembly and read the Gospel pericope while standing below the chandelier. After having finished, he headed toward the prince and his son and had them kiss it, then he kissed their hands. During the incensing, at the Cherubic Hymn, my lord the patriarch went out of the sanctuary with the censer; he placed himself between the candlesticks, incensed the prince and the assistants, then he turned to incense the doors of the sanctuary and the icons and re-entered the sanctuary. I went out with the paten and the Holy Body. A carpet of silk fabric embroidered with gold, having in the center a motif in red velvet, was then spread before the prince's throne; another carpet of the same shape, but smaller, was also spread before the throne of his son. They descended and prostrated themselves on these carpets. When I arrived near them, I turned toward them according to custom, saying: "May the Lord always remember in His heavenly kingdom the prince Ioanu the Voivode Basil, his wife the princess Catherine, and his son the Voivode Stephen". The priest who carried the chalice did the same. My lord the patriarch had removed his mitre, which one of the priests


[p. 457] {#po.24.p457}
مع الجند حسب عادتهم كل من بيحمل بيده شي؛ ولو انه قنداق القداس وفي حضور البيك كما ذكرنا جهدهم بيقتصروا في الصلاه وعلي الكينويكون! نزل لبيك" تارك من الايقونات كالعاده كذلك واينه بعد وطلع لكريبيه اعلم ان الكاهن في قوله افشين بارك الذين يباركونك يارب دايمًا بيذكر البيك وحرمته وابنه في كل هذه البلاد ثم الفلاخ والقزرق الي المصكودٌ ثم خرج سيدنا البطرك وخرج الكاهن والشماس بض على صينية السليقه الملوكيه التي احضرها البيك مع وعا فضه خمر لان عادته كلما حضر قداس بيكون الجميع منه حتي والطراباذا وصلي عليها سيدنا الطرك افشين كلعادة دعا له ولحرمته ولابنه ثم ان البيك نزل من كرسيه وتناول من يده الانديدارا وكذلك ابنه ووقفوا في كراسييم ان بقية البيوراية والاكابر. الخواص اتوا اخدوا ايض واحد بعد واحد وتاركوا من الايقونات” حب العادة وخرج الكاهن بصينيه اخري فرق على الذين خارج ثم اتوا حينيذ بصينية السليقه لقدام البيك ووضع سيدنا البطرك يده عليها مع ايادي رؤس الكبنة وبقية الكبنه
carried before the Holy Body according to their custom: everyone carried something in his hand, if only a Mass book. The presence of the prince, as we have said, forced them to shorten the office. At the "Kinonikon" he descended to kiss the icons, and his son after him; he then returned to his throne. Remark. When the priest says the prayer: "Bless, O Lord, those who bless You", he always mentions the prince, his wife, and his son; which is done in all this country, in Wallachia, and among the Cossacks as far as Moscow. My lord the patriarch, the priest, and the deacon went out to incense the tray of princely colyva which had been brought by the prince with a silver vase containing wine. It was his custom when he attended Mass that everything should be provided by him, as well as the meal at his table. My lord the patriarch said a prayer over these offerings and he prayed for the prince, for his wife, and for his son. After that, the prince descended from his throne, received the blessed bread from his hand, as did his son; they placed themselves on their thrones; then the other boyars, the principal notables, came one after the other to receive blessed bread as well, and they kissed the icons. The priest then went out with another tray and he distributed the blessed bread to those who were outside, then the tray of colyva was brought before the prince. My lord the patriarch, the archpriests, and the priests placed their hands on the


[p. 458] {#po.24.p458}
الحاضرين ودعاء للبيك! مقدمها كثيراً كلعاده ثم ان سيدنا البطرك_ممناول منها اذل وثانبً البيك وابنه وبقية الحاضرين ثم شريوا من وعا الخمر كذلك ثم ان سيدنا البطرك وقف تحت البوليا لاون ودعاء للبيك بترجمان كيرا ثم تم الصلاه ودخل للبيكل قلع بدلته واولاد الخزنه قالوا بولبخرونيون البيك الى ان فرغ سيدا البطرك_ لسنلا منثيه والبيك واقف بكرسيه ثم خرج ونحن معه الي خارج باب الكنيسه ووقف حتي خرج البيك بارك عليه حسب العادة دايمً وعلي جميع من كان فى الكليسه ثم صعد هو واياا الى خلون اول يتخاطبوا الى ان صمدوا الطرابازا وقاموا الها وكما قلنا ان حيث ما حضر اليك قداس بتكون الطراباذا والخمر شيء يكفي هل قدر الو عساكر وخلايق منه وحين العصر نزلوا للكنيسه وصلوا الغروب ثم خرج سيدنا اللطرك قدامه اإضا حتي بارك عليه في خروجه وركب في قوحيته وخرج وانواقيس تقرع الي ان غاب وتحقيق ان كان نار يعد من الاعمار كما ذكرنا اول وما كان يشيقني الا صلاة وترئيم
colyva, then the patriarch prayed at length for the prince who had offered these gifts. My lord the patriarch took of it first, then the prince and his son as well as the other assistants; they then drank from the vase containing the wine. My lord the patriarch, standing below the chandelier, prayed for the prince with an interpreter and he ended the service. He entered the sanctuary, removed his vestments, and the children of the princely house said the polychronion of the prince, until my lord the patriarch had finished putting on the "mandyas", while the prince was standing at his throne; then he went out accompanied by us to the door of the church. He remained there until the prince went out; he then blessed him according to custom, as well as all those who were in the church. Then he went up with the prince, first into a secluded salon where they chatted together, until the table had been set; after which they headed toward the refectory. As we have said, each time the prince attends Mass, the dishes and the wine would be sufficient for a few thousand soldiers and people. In the afternoon, they descended to the church, heard vespers, and my lord the patriarch, always walking in front of him, blessed him at the exit. The prince left in his carriage and the bells rang until he was out of sight. In truth, this day was a day that deserves to be counted in one's life, as we have said above. Nothing pleased me as much as the prayer and the singing of the young men of the princely house and their application to surpass one another.


[p. 459] {#po.24.p459}
اولاد الخزنه ومغايرتهم لبعضهم بعض ثم عاودنا لديرا ونظرنا في طريقنا الحرةة العظيمة التي في وري صرايا البيك المسماا خالستاو وهني مجمع السمك الخاص المسما افنديكو متحف قوي وفي جوفه بزر خيارى كانت مجلده في هذه اليوم والخاله بخلبم ماشيين فوقرا :من االشرطه "الى دي :الغلطة وإذا ازادوا يملوا منها ماك ا يوا الدواب يحثروا ابالقوين صفة بير من الجليد الي ان تخرج الماء ويستقوا ثم تجلد ايض وكذلك كانوا يفعلون الصيادون اذا ارادوا ان يصطادوا له منها سنك بالحيله وأهدوا لنا فى هذه اليوم فاح كياد حسن رابحه ولون وطعم ويسمود فاسيليكو اي ملوكي بعالو في هذه اللاد في المغاير بين الحشيش وقيم الي ايام تفاح الجديد وكذلك اهدوا لنا دراقن وخوخ وكراز مكبوس في حلو لا تحسب ألا انه مقطوع جديد ولديد
We then returned to the convent. On our way, we noticed a large pond called "helesteu": it is located behind the prince's palace. It is a fishpond for very rare fish called "effendico", esteemed, having caviar in their bellies. That day, the pond was frozen and the horsemen passed over it with their horses to go from the palace to the convent of Galata. When one wants to draw water from it or water the mounts, one breaks the ice with blows of axes in the shape of a well until the water rises; one waters the horses, then the water freezes again. The fishermen do the same when they want to catch fish for the prince. That day, we were offered large, fragrant apples of beautiful color, delicious to the taste, called "vasiliko", that is to say, princely. In this country, they are kept in cellars on straw and they keep until the new apple season. They also presented us with peaches, plums, and cherries preserved in syrup, so fresh and so tasty that one would have thought they had been picked very recently. XVII. - The Divine Office in the other weeks of Lent.
ووم -الأشنين الثانى من الصوم. دقو -ناقوس: السواعى بعد الساعه السادسة وخرجنا اقيل الساعة الثامنه وعلي هذا القانون لوا جميع الصوم وقرب دين مار سابا دين على اسم القديسه بارسكافي وكنيسه لطيفه «هو في .يد
On the second Monday of Lent, the bell was rung for the hours after the sixth hour, and we went out before the eighth hour. It is according to this rule that the office was performed throughout Lent. Near the convent of Saint Sabas is that of Saint Paraskevi with a beautiful church. This latter convent is occupied by monks from Mount Sinai.


[p. 460] {#po.24.p460}
وهبان طود سين وايقونه المت كاترينا وقية الابوئات مع القوتطاى جميعه وابواب الياكل والرسل شغل بلاد الروى ونصور على حابط باب الكنيسه جميعه صودة جيل الله طود سينا والدير وجبل موسي حيث تلما الادي وجي الست كاترنا وقية الديارة هناك والعريان وغير ذلك كمثل ما هو معين بعينه فهذا لهم ودير مار ميخايل مقابل دين الفلطه واما كنيسة ماد نقولا التي بقرب القرله فبي تغبه الكنيسه التي في فاسيلودي من عمارة ستافانوس ونوضه منظبه كيرد كباب اليه وخارحا عمارة ملاصتها .ذكروا إنا عمارة الترك وذكروا لنا فى هددا مديئة .ياش ثلانون كنيسه ودير وهى بلده شرحه وفواها وباها لديد عذب جد احسن من ساير هذه البلاد وكذلك قداس سبت التانى من الصوم والاسبارنون عشية ثم سحر الاحد الثاني والقدان حضرتلا في كنيسة الدير ووم الارسا من الجمعه الثالشه من الصوم الني! تاسع دار وعيد الارسين شاهد خرجنا من قداس البروجياسمانا بعد الساعة السابعة ونصف يكون انقص عن العادة نصف ساعه لاغير
Each iconostasis has the icon of Saint Catherine and the other icons. The doors of the sanctuaries and the images of the apostles were executed in Russia. On the wall of the church door, one sees paintings representing the mountain of God, Mount Sinai, the convent, the mountain of Moses where he spoke with the Creator, Mount Saint Catherine, and the other monasteries of this region, with Bedouins, etc. One would think one was seeing these places in reality. This belongs to them. The convent of Saint Michael is located opposite the convent of Galata. The church of Saint Nicholas which is near the palace resembles that of Vaslui which was built at the expense of the Voivode Stephen. It is majestic and large with high domes * and on the outside there is a building that is contiguous to it; it is said that it is a construction of the Turks. We were assured that in this city of Jassy there are thirty churches and convents. The aspect offered by the city is pleasant, the air is pure, the waters are sweet and healthy, better than those of all the other cities in this country. On the second Saturday of Lent, we celebrated Mass and in the evening we sang vespers. On the morning of the second Sunday, we said Mass in the church of the convent. On Wednesday of the third week of Lent, that is to say, the ninth of March, the feast of the "forty martyrs", we went out from the Mass of the Presanctified after the seventh hour and a half, that is to say, only half an hour less than usual.


[p. 461] {#po.24.p461}
واحضروا للكنيسة قداسات كثير وصحون خبز كعك اشكال والوان وشمع كثير وسايقه وفرقوا منهم بركه على الحاضرين كالعادة وصاح الاحد اثالث من الصوم حين باصا بنوبي عند اخر آجيوس خرج الكاهن من اليكل وهو حامل على راسه صينيه فيها الصليب وقدامه الشماس بالميضة والشمع وهو يهتد فللا الى ان اتا الى امام قراية الانجيل التى كانت وضعوها تحت الوليالاون مجلّله بتر مخمل منقوش وروازد مخمل اخضر بشراريب من تحت ثم وضعه عليها واخد المبخرة من الشماس وض حوليا وهو مصليًً تممقم)وم»2 دده موجه دؤما الى ثمام الدور م ترنمهاة ثانيًٍ الي تمام الدور الثاني ثم ترنمها اث الي تمام الدور الثالث ثم نزل سيدنا البطرك سجد نحو الصليب ثلاث مطائيات كبار وتبارك منه وبارك به على الشعب كالعاده ثم دخلوا بعده الريشيسا والكبنه وبقية الشعب ائنين اثنين عملوا كذلك وخرنا من الكنيسه وكان حضرة البيك قد ارسل له خبر من عشية ايض حسب علدت انه يبات
On this day, the people brought to the church a large quantity of small round loaves, ordinary bread, cakes, and pretzels of all shapes; they also brought many candles and colyva. They were distributed as a blessing to the assistants, as is the custom. On the morning of the third Sunday of Lent, during the singing of: "Let everything that breathes" at the end of "Holy God", the priest went out of the sanctuary, carrying above his head a tray on which the cross had been placed, and the deacon walked before him with the censer and the candle. He advanced slowly until he had arrived before the tetrapodion of the Gospel book placed below the chandelier; the tetrapodion was covered with an embroidered velvet carpet with a green velvet hem and embroidered with fringes. The priest placed the tray there, took the censer from the hands of the deacon, incensed all around, singing: "Your cross" until he had finished the round; he sang it for the second time until the end of the second round, then he sang it again a third time, until the end of the third round. My lord the patriarch then descended, made three great genuflections before the cross, kissed it, then he blessed the faithful with the cross according to custom. After him, the hegumens, the priests, and the rest of the people entered two by two and did the same. We went out of the church. As is his custom, His Highness the prince let the patriarch know from the previous evening to prepare himself to


[p. 462] {#po.24.p462}
على قداس في دين الضوينا الذي ذكرئاا ثم ارسل له قوجيه بنت رون خيل حمر ومضينا ودخانا للكنيسه وابدلنا وليسناد كالعادة وتاركنا من صليب كبير مذهب داخله من صليب سيدنا يسوع المسيح لونه اسود ما به غيا وهو وقف علي جيل التقدس وعياره مودوع في هذا الدينر لان ديرين البيك هولاء! اوقفيم علي جيل المقدن ورشيساهم ورهبابم منه وحين اقبل البيك دقوا له جميع النواقيس وحاء في عراشه ابهج من ذاك اليوم وخرحنا لاستقباله حسب العاده وكرسه بالأجيازس كما ذكرنا" ثم كمانا القداس الالبي وأكلنا من السليقه ايض وخرج سيدنا البطرك كذلك قدامه حتى بارك عليه وركب فى القوجيه ووقف الي ان ركب سيدنا البطرك في قوجيته وبشوا جملة الي القرطه وعمل معه هذا اليوم اتشراح وصقاة كلي علي الطراباذا واوعده يعمل تاج ذهب وحله كبنوتيه كامله والمسا عاودنا وليله احد الرابع من الصوم عشيه ارسل له خبر انه يبات علي قداس في
celebrate a Mass the next day in the princess's convent, the convent of which we spoke above. He then sent him a carriage with six bay horses and we left. Having arrived at the church, we put on our vestments and vested the patriarch, we kissed the large golden cross inside which there is a piece, black in color, of the True Cross of our Lord Jesus Christ. This relic belongs to Mount Athos and the document attesting to the authenticity of the wood (of the True Cross) is deposited in the convent, because these two convents of the prince were bequeathed to Mount Athos. The hegumens and the monks of these convents come from there. At the arrival of the prince, all the bells were rung in his honor; he came in a more grandiose apparatus than the previous time. As usual, we went out to meet him, and the patriarch sprinkled him with holy water. We finished the holy Mass and we also ate colyva. My lord the patriarch went out to meet him to bless him; the prince got back into the carriage and waited until my lord the patriarch had gotten into his, and they left together to go to the palace. That day the prince treated him at the table with all the cordiality and all the affability possible. He promised him to have a gold mitre and a complete priestly vestment made for him. In the evening we returned. On the eve of the fourth Sunday of Lent, the prince sent


[p. 463] {#po.24.p463}
دير برتوصكه! وباكر بعد الاورة ن ارسل له القوحية ومضي الي الدين المدكور ولسنلا ثم دقت النواقيس وحاء البيك في عراضه احسن واجمل راكب علي فرس اشبب تركي عليه من الزركش والمرصعات كثير وخرحنا لاستقاله كالعاده ودخلنا كملنا القداس وانا قات الانجيل في هذا اليوم ثم قبلته اياد وقبات يمينه ولابنه ايض وفي ساير هده البلاد كانوا يتعجبوا من قرااتنا بالرومي كثير لان اسمنا عندهم حقير ثم بعد ان فرق سيدنا البطرك الانديدارا وإكلنا من السليقه وخرج بمنثيته قدامه وبادك عليه وكان موجود فى هذا الوم ادبع مطارنه اسقف القرطه الدايم ومطران صوفيا وفلاسيوس مطران تافاكطوس في بلاد
word to my lord the patriarch to prepare to celebrate Mass in the convent of Barnovski. In the morning after matins, he sent him the carriage and the patriarch went to this convent, and we vested him in the priestly ornaments. Then the bells were rung and the prince arrived in a procession even more beautiful than previously; he was riding a gray Turkish horse, covered with a brocade of gold thread inlaid with precious stones. We went out to meet him and, re-entering the church, we finished the Mass. That day, it was I who read the "pericope" of the Gospel, after which I presented the book to him to kiss, * I kissed his right hand and that of his son. In all these countries, people were very surprised by our way of reading Greek, for among them our name is little considered. After my lord the patriarch had distributed the blessed bread and we had eaten the "colyva", he went out before the prince vested in the "mandyas" and he blessed him. That day, four metropolitans had attended the office, namely: the permanent bishop of the palace, the metropolitan of Sophia, Blaise, metropolitan of Naupactus in


[p. 464] {#po.24.p464}
موره واسقف من الكرج كان حا من المصكوف جديد بحسنه وافره زعم انه اسقف على كنيسة فى بلاد الداديان بها قميص السيده والدة الآله ولاجل ذلك اعطاا الملك غير الصدقه الوافره تاج كاليستري وساير حلة رياسة الكبنوت وكان تلبيس يلبس قميص شعر مبح ظاهراً ثم اخذوا سيدنا البطرك بالقوجية معهم الى القرطه للطراباذا الملوكيه ايض والمسا عاودنا للدين وليلة خميس النوبنه عملوا سهرانه عظيمه ودقوا النواقيس فى الساعة
Morea, and the bishop of Georgia; the latter had come recently from Moscow with abundant alms. He claimed to be the bishop of a church in the land of Dadiane where the tunic of the holy Virgin, Mother of God, is kept. Because of this, the emperor of Moscow gave him, in addition to abundant alms, a pretty mitre and a complete pontifical vestment. He was dressed externally in a hair shirt. In the company of these prelates, my lord the patriarch was conducted to the palace for the princely meal; in the evening we returned to the convent. On the eve of the Thursday of Penitence, the great vigils were celebrated; the bells were rung at two hours


[p. 465] {#po.24.p465}
الثانية من الليل فأولاً دق القندلفت ناقون الخشب تلثمايه مره عدد وعند كل ثمام مايه كان يقف ثم دقوا نواقيس النحاس جميعها كثيراً ثم دخلنا للكنيسه وطلع الكاهن بخر من غير بدو صلا من سكوت ثم وقف تحت الخورص وقال كيريه ابلوجيصن! ثم تم في بخور سيدنا اللطرك3 ودخل للبيكل من باب الملوكى وقال جارة 8+ سيد" كالعاده وقال سيدنا البطرك ستة مزامير من غين قدوس الله اول القطع الثلائه ثم الليلويا وتريصاجيون وقالوا كاتسما من المزامير ثم ارحمني يا الله ويدوا في القانون جميعه بترم لديد لان العاده فى جميع هذد البلاد ان المتقدم يقول مزامير السحريه ثم المجد المرسل_النور والمجد له في العلاء وتوابعها وابها المسيح الضو الحقانى ثم مزمور الغروب وايا النور لبي واهلنا والان اطلق وعد الفوطاغوحيكا كملوا الصلاه مع الساعة الاولى كالعاده وخرجنا من الصلاه بعد الساعة السادسه من الليل وعد الظهر قدسوا بروبجياسمانا ووضعوا اقونة البشار المتفق فى الغد عيده ونزل سيدنا البطرك تارك منها كالعادة وقية الحاضرين بعد
of the night. The sacristan first struck the simandron three hundred times and, at the end of each hundred strokes, he stopped; then he rang all the bronze bells several times; finally, we entered the church. The priest began to incense in silence, without starting the office; he stopped below the choir and said: "Bless, Master". Then he finished the incensing of my lord the patriarch, entered the sanctuary through the royal door, and said: "Glory to the holy [Trinity]" according to custom. My lord the patriarch recited six psalms without saying "Holy God" at the beginning, three troparia, then "alleluia", and the "trisagion". A "kathisma" of the psalms was recited and "God, have mercy on me", then the entire canon was begun in a harmonious melody. It is the custom in this country that the one who presides says the morning psalms, then "Glory to Him who sent us the light" and "Glory to God in the highest" and "O Christ, true light", then the vesper psalms and "O gladsome light", then "Vouchsafe, O Lord" and "Now let Your servant depart" and after, the "photagogika". We finished the office with the first hour according to usage, and we left the service after the sixth hour of the night. In the afternoon, they had celebrated the Mass of the Presanctified and they exposed the icon of the Annunciation, whose feast was to be celebrated the next day. My lord the patriarch descended, kissed it, as did all the other assistants. After having received the blessed bread, we


[p. 466] {#po.24.p466}
الانديدارا ثم خرحنا للطرابازا وشرينا خمر وما صار فى هذ الليلة صلانة نوم ولا دقوا نواقيسن! ولكن ليلة الجمعه نصف ليل دقوا النواقيس ودخلنا الكنيسه وبدوا فى صلاة النوم الكبيره وعند ارحمنا يارب ارحمنا بدوا فى الليتين وحطوا خمسة خزات ووعا زت ووعا خمر ووعا قمح وشمعدانين فوق القرايه تحت الخورص وقال الشماس بعد البخور طلبة الاغرنيه خلص يا الله شعبك وتوابعها ثم بخر حول الاغرنيه واخد واحده منها بارك عليها سيدنا الطرك ثم ابتدوا في السحريه السشة مزامير من غير قدو الله والقطع الثلئه ثم قالوا تاوس كيريوس والمزامير والقانون وما قالوا التسابيح ولكن عوضهم يا والدة الاله خلصينا وبعد باصا بنوبي نزل سيدنا اللطرك تبارك من ابقونة البشارة والحاضرين وخرجا من الاورترون سحر وكان البيك ادسل له خبر انه يبات على قداس فى كنيسة القديس مار نقلاة بقرب القرطه المشهورة بكنيسة الباكات ثم ارسل له القوجيه ومضينا اليها وابدلنا ثم
went to sit at the table and we drank wine. During the evening, the service of compline was not performed and the bells were not rung. But on Friday at midnight, the bells were rung, we entered the church, and the service of the great compline was begun, and after "Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us" the "litia" was begun. Five loaves, a vase of oil, one of wine, and one of wheat, and two candlesticks were placed on the "tetrapodion" below the choir. After having incensed, the deacon said the prayer of the vigils: "Save, O Lord, Your people", etc. ... and he incensed around the loaves; he took one and my lord the patriarch blessed it. At matins, the six psalms were begun without saying "Holy God" and the three pieces; they then said: "God is the Lord", the psalms, and the canon. They did not recite the canticles but in their place they said: "O Virgin, Mother of God, save us". After "Let everything that breathes", my lord the patriarch descended, kissed the icon of the Annunciation, which the assistants also did. We left matins early. The prince sent word to the patriarch to prepare to celebrate Mass in the church of Saint Nicholas, near the palace, known by the name of the church * of the princes. He sent him his carriage and we left. We put on our vestments as well as my lord the patriarch, and we


[p. 467] {#po.24.p467}
ولسيدنا البطرك وابتدينا فى القداس لان البيك كذا اراد ثم قلت افلوجيصن دسبوطا والكاهن افلوجيماني وبدوا في صلاة الغروب بادكي يا نسي للرب ثم السينابتي ثم القطع المعلومه ثم الايصودن وحينيشد دخل البيك وكان ماشي من الصرايا وما لاقينا له حب العادة لاجل بدونا القداس ولكن فى حال وقوفه بكرسيه بارك عليه وعلى ابنه ثانا وثالث ثم كمانا القداس واكلنا من السليقة الملوكية بعد الافشين دعا للبيك كما ذكرنا ومن الخمر ثم وقف البيك بكرسيه الى بينما نزع سيدنا اللبطرك بدلته وليسناه منثيته حسب العادة كما ذكرنا وخرج قدامه الى ان بارك عليه ومشى هو واياا الى الصرايا وصار فى هذا اليوم طرابانا واحتفال أكثر! من العادة ببب أنه عيد سيّدي هذا ولما كانت تخرج الصحون من المطبخ الى فوق كانت تدق الطبول وتزعق النفيرات والزمور وصار في هذا اليوم فرح كثير والنوبة السلطانية وغنا الترك لا يبطل الي المسآ واتينا بالقوجية الى الدير
began the Mass according to the desire of the prince. I said: "Bless, Master" and the priest said: "Blessed be". The vesper psalm was begun: "Bless the Lord, O my soul!", the litany and the known pieces, then the prince, who was coming on foot from the palace, entered at that moment. We did not go to meet him as usual, because we had begun the Mass, but when he had placed himself on his throne, the patriarch blessed him as well as his son, two, three times, then we finished the Mass. We ate the "royal colyva", and after the conclusion, the patriarch prayed for the prince and we drank wine. The prince stood on his throne waiting for my lord the patriarch to have removed his vestments and for us to have vested him in the "mandyas" according to custom. The patriarch went out before him to bless him and they walked together to the palace. That day there was a feast and a solemnity greater than usual, because it was a feast of our Lord. When the dishes were brought up from the kitchen to the dining room, the drum was beaten, the trumpet was sounded, and the flute was played. There was great rejoicing; the Turkish music and the Turkish songs did not cease until evening. We returned to the convent by carriage.
اعلم ان زرع شجر الدراقن وغير ثم الورد وغيره ثم بدار القول والبصل والثوم وغير ذلك بيكون فى اواسط شهر ادار واما الليمون فى هذ الايام بيجلبوه من الرميله ويطمرؤا
Remark. In the middle of the month of March, peach trees and rose bushes are planted and fava beans, onions, garlic, etc., are sown. At this time, lemons are imported from Rumelia and they are placed in the cellars, wrapped


[p. 468] {#po.24.p468}
فى المغر وكل واحده بشاهيه وكل اقه رمان نصف غرش مجلوب لان ما فى هذه الديار شى واحدة! وما صار فى هذه ليلة سبت المدايح لا دق اقوس ولا صلاة غررب ولكن بعد دق الساعة السابعه من الليل عملوا ايض سهرانه عظيمه ودقوا ناقون الخشب تلثماية مر كما ذكرنا ثم حرص الكبير مايه وعد بخور الكاهن ومزامير البحرة وتاون كيريوس وكاتسما من المزامير وضعوا القرايه المجللة قدام ايقونة السّدة وشعلوا شموع الشماعدين ثم نزل سيدنا البطرك من كرسيه ووضع البطراشيل والاموفرون وقال ستة بيوت من المدايح وطلع لكرسيه ثم قالوا كاتسما ثانيه من المزامير ثم قال الاسقف ستة بيوت اخري كانوا يعيدوا الارموس ثم قال رييس الدير سته ثم التيماريوس ستة وتربموا القانون خلواً من تسابيح ايض ولكن باوالدة الاله خلصينا ثم خرحنا من الاودترن سح وفي هذا اليوم الني هو سبت المدايح قدس سيدنا اللطرك فى كنيسة مار سابال المدكورة لكل الازركان كان قداس محتفل قوي وعملنا طقسنا بتمامه وعد ان خرجنا من القداس قرب الفلهر ولسناه المنثية كالعاده وخرحنا من الكنيسه ومضوا بالشموع قدامه الى حيث
in paper. An oka of imported pomegranates costs half a piastre, for in this region there are none. On the eve of the Saturday of the Akathist, the bells were not rung and vespers were not celebrated. After seven o'clock in the evening had struck, great vigils were performed and the simandron was struck three hundred times, as we have said, then the great bell was rung one hundred times. After the priest had incensed and the morning psalms had been read and "God is the Lord" had been said, the "kathisma" of the psalms, the "triskelion" covered with a carpet was placed before the icon of the holy Virgin, and after the candles of the candlesticks had been lit, my lord the patriarch descended from his throne, put on the stole and the "omophorion", and said six strophes of the "Akathist", then he returned to his throne. The second kathisma of the psalms was then recited, then the bishop said six other strophes - they repeated the "hirmos" - the hegumen said six, the officiating priest, after him, recited six others. They also sang the canon without canticles and the invocation "O Mother of God, save us". We finished matins early in the morning. That day, Saturday of the Akathist, my lord celebrated Mass in the said church of Saint Sabas, for the merchants; it was an extremely solemn Mass and we performed our entire office. After having left the Mass, it was nearly noon, we vested the patriarch in the "mandyas" and we left the church. One walked before


[p. 469] {#po.24.p469}
المقابر خارحها لان رجل طلب من سيدنا اللطرك قابلًا ان ابي قبل ما يتوفي اصاني قايلا ان بعد موتي الي مدة ثلاث سنين افتح قبري وابصر رشيس كبنة حاض او ان اتفق بطرك حتى يقرا على افشين استغفار وحلّ فليستاا حينيشدٍ البطداشيل والاموفوون وبدا الصلاة
him with candles to the cemetery outside the church, for a man had said to my lord the patriarch: "My father, before dying, gave me these recommendations: 'three years after my death, open my tomb, and look in the presence of a high dignitary, and if there is a patriarch, let him read the absolution over me.'" - We then put the stole and the omophorion on him and he began the service.

### XVIII. Funerals in Moldavia
وترنموا قطع الاموات المعلومة ثم قال افاشين الحل ثم السبخوروخاتي اي الاستغفار وطرش على أعضلا من الاجيازمس ثم اتنا الى الطراباذا الدير وعملوا له اهله منيموسينون اي تذكار وذلك انهم احضروا للطرابازا مايده كبيره من ساير الطعامات ثم سليقه وخمر وفرقوا على الحاضرين كليم شمع كبار ثم قام3 سيدنا البطرك مع بقية الحاضرين قايلًا افلوجيطوس أو تاوس ايمون ثم قدوس الله وترنموا قطع الاموات ثم بض الشماس وهو باستبخاري بسخرد فضه بلا سناسل بقبضه لكل الحاضرين وقال ارحمنا يا الله كعظيم رحمتك ثم من اجل ناح نفس عبد الله فلان الراقد بتمامها وقال سيدنا
The usual prayers for the dead were sung; the patriarch then said the absolution and "syghoreite", * that is to say, the prayer for the remission of sins, after which he sprinkled the body with holy water. Then we went to the refectory of the convent where the family held a "mnimosynon" or requiem for him. In the refectory, a large table was set with all kinds of dishes, to which were added colyva and wine, distributing large candles to all the assistants. My lord the patriarch then stood up as well as the other assistants and said: "Blessed be our God", then "Holy God" and they sang the strophes for the dead. The deacon, vested in the alb, incensed all the assistants with the silver censer and said: "Have mercy on us, O God, according to Your great mercy"


[p. 470] {#po.24.p470}
اللطرك افشين الموتى يا الا الارواح ثم ختموا الصلاة واطفوا الشموع وتناولوا جميعبهم من السليقه وترحمنا عليه ود ان تخلصنا من الغدا فرق ابنه على كل الحاضرين حسنه لان هذه عدتبم في بلاد البغضان والفلاخ وهذا طقس المنيموسينون ونحن منسميه الصباحيه والثالث والتاسع ولما قمنا عن المايده حينيشد دقوا ناقوس الكبير وذلك علامه عندهم لتجتمع كبنة البلدة الي جنازته هذا اذا كان غني اي ان احدهم! مات وفي الدين الذي مرادهم اهله ان يدفنود بيامروا القندلفت بقرعه مستور الحال واما اذا كان فقير بيدفنوه بلا ناقوس لكي لا يدري احدًا ثم مضي من الدير ستة كبنة بافلونياتهم وشماسان بالمباخر الى بيته لياتوا به وعادتهم فى هذ البلاد اذا مات ميت ما بيقبرؤا ليومين ثلثه ومع هذا فغير كفن مكشوف الوجه ويشعلوا حوله الشموع ليلا ونباداً واي من اراد من الكبنة ان مضى الى عنده وشرا عليه بالانجيل من اوله لا خرا لامرلا واحده ولكن مرات عديده من حين وفاته الي حين دقنه ولو كانوا خمسون كاهن او شمامسه وذلك لاحل الحسنة الوافرة
then "for the repose of the soul of the servant of God, the deceased so-and-so" until the end. My lord the patriarch said the prayer for the dead: "O God of spirits". The service was then finished and the candles were extinguished. All took of the "colyva" and we prayed for the dead. After lunch, the son of the deceased distributed alms among the assistants, because it is the custom in the lands of Moldavia and Wallachia and such is the rite of the requiem that we call "sabahie" and the service of the third and ninth day. When we rose from the table, the great bell was rung: among them, it is the sign that someone has died. If the family desires that he be buried in the convent, it gives the order to the sacristan to ring the bells to assemble for the funeral all the priests of the city. This happens if he is rich or of average situation; but if he is poor, they bury him without ringing the bells, so that the death remains unknown to all. After that, six priests of the convent in chasubles went to the house of the deceased as well as two deacons with censers, to bring the body. The custom among them is, if someone happens to die, not to bury him until the end of two or three days, without a shroud, with the face uncovered, lighting candles day and night around the dead. Any priest who desires to come to his house may do so, to read "pericopes" of the Gospel, from the beginning to the end, not only once, but several times, from the moment of death until that of the burial, even if there were fifty priests or deacons, and this is done for abundant alms


[p. 471] {#po.24.p471}
فهذا المرحوم الذي توفي كان رجل اختيار صاحنا واصله من قونيه من آنا وجدود نكجاريه وهو كان انكجاري. ايض فجا الي هنا واعتمد وتزوج وكان الختمان اخو البيك اي الوزير صار اشبينه واما الديانه التى كانت عنده ومواضته الصلاه فما كانت ولا عند واحد منا فحين توفي كان عليه ديون لبازركان ترك فارسل للحال الخمان ختم علي اسبابه واخرجوا منها! فتيح لتجبيزه ودفنه نحو عشرون دهب وفيما بعد يبقوا المداينيه يتقاسموا رزقه وأرسل وصا كل الازركان ان يحتفلوا بجنازته بسبب توبيخ الترك الحاضرين فلما كانوا جايين بنعشه والكهنة قدامه والشموع كلما وصلوا الي عند اوضة الاتراك كانوا بوضهولة هنلك: وصلوا عليه :بحن عالى الى أن دخلوا ية للكئيسة ووضعوا فى الوسظ هذا والناقون يقرع ثم سكت ثم لسنا أسيدنا البطرك المنثيه ودخلنا للكنيسة ثم وضعنا له اللطراشيل والاموفورن ووقف عند راس المايت ووجهه مكشوف والايقونه علي صدره ثم ان الشماس ناوله المبخر حسب عادتهم فبجر حول المايت شكل صليب ثم لابواب اليكل وهو قايلًا افلوجيطوس وابتدوا بالجناز ثم لخورصس اليمين والشمال ولقية الكبنة
As for the deceased, he was an elderly man and our friend, a native of Konia (Iconium), son and grandson of janissaries. He had been, himself, a janissary. He had come here, had been baptized, and had married. The hatman, brother of the prince and his vizier, had been his godfather. His piety and his assiduity in prayer were not met with in any of us. When he died, he owed money to Turkish merchants. The hatman immediately sent to place seals on his goods. About twenty ducats were taken officially for the expenses of his funeral and his burial; after that, the creditors shared his goods. He had recommended to all the merchants to attend his funeral procession and his burial because of the blame that the Turks present were addressing [to the deceased]. When the bier was brought, the priests walked in front carrying candles. When one arrived at a Turkish house, it was placed on the ground and the priests prayed in a loud voice until they had introduced it into the church and placed it in the middle, while the bells rang. Then silence was made. We vested my lord the patriarch in the "mandyas" and we entered the church; we put the stole and the "omophorion" on him. He stood near the head of the dead man whose face was uncovered and who had an icon on his chest, and according to custom the deacon handed the censer to the patriarch and the latter incensed around the dead in the shape of a cross, then he incensed the doors of the sanctuary saying: "Blessed", then the service of the absolution was begun. He incensed the right choir and the left one and all * the priests who were there as well as


[p. 472] {#po.24.p472}
الموجودين والشعب ثم بخر حول النعش ثانا والتفت نحو المشرق وصلب علي وه ثلاث مرار ثم طلع لكرسيه وهم عمالين! يترنموا طوباهم وارحمني يا الله بترئيم وتطريح مناقله ثم القانون كذلك واول علان قاله سيدنا البطرك ثم ان الاسقف الحاض اخد المبخرة بعد سيدنا البطرك وبض حول المايت اول وثانيًا ثم لابواب البيكل ولبقية الحاضرين وصلب على وحهه ايض وعمل كارون اول وثانياً لسيدنا البطرك ووقف مكانه وقال. الاعلان الثاني في حينه وهكذا فعلوا جميع الكبنة الحاضرين علي مراتهم الي حين التقبيل ثم في آخرهم الشماس الخادم وكان هو يمضي بالمبخرة نحو الكاهن على مرتته وبخره مرتين ثلثه حتى يخرج وتناولبا منه وبعد عودته كان الكاهن يبخر الشماس مرتين ثاثه كذلك ونارله اهى وكل كاهن يبح ركان يقول “الاعلان المتتقبل-فى: حينه الى: الثمام -ولأبد عن بخود كرسي البيك دايمً ثم نزل سيدنا البطرك الي عند راسه وقرا الانجيل والعاده ان حرمته واولاده واهله بيسجدوا تحت الانجيل وهو يقرا ثم قرا عليه افشين الحل وتقدم
the laypeople, then he incensed around the bier a second time and turned toward the east, made the sign of the cross three times on his face, then he mounted to his throne, while they sang "Blessed are those who" and "Have mercy on me, O God" with the alternating chant as well as the canon. My lord the patriarch recited the first conclusion. Then the bishop present took the censer after my lord the patriarch and incensed around the dead, a first and a second time, then he incensed the doors of the sanctuary and the assistants and he made the sign of the cross on his face, then he bowed twice toward my lord the patriarch and he stopped at his place and at the appropriate moment, he recited the second conclusion, then the other priests present, according to the rank of precedence, did the same until the moment when one gives a kiss to the dead. After them, the officiating deacon went with the censer before each priest according to their rank, he incensed him two or three times until he went out; the priest in his turn took the censer from him, incensed the deacon two or three times in the same way, and he handed the censer back to him. Each priest who incensed said the following conclusion at the proper time, until the end, without ever omitting the incensing of the prince's throne. My lord the patriarch descended to the head of the dead man and read the Gospel. It is customary that the wife, the children, and the relatives of the deceased kneel under the Gospel book during the reading of the "pericope". After that, he read the absolution of the dead and advanced toward the icon that was on the chest of the deceased, made three genuflections toward the east, and kissed the icon; he made


[p. 473] {#po.24.p473}
الى عند الابقوته التي على صدر وعمل نحو المشرق ثلاث مطانيات ثم قلا ثم ثلاث اخري وطلع لكرسيه ثم جا الاسقف وريس الدير كل واحد من جبة وعملوا مطائيات نحو المشرق وقبلوا الايقونة ايض ثم حأآوا الكمهنة كذلك والشمامسه والرهبان اثنان اثنان وكان واقف من اهالي او محبين المايت اثنان من الناحيتان يفرقوا علي الجماعه باسرهم حسنه في اوراق او في محادم مطرزة الى الفقراء ثم دخلوا الاعيان الي اخر الجماعه علي مراتهم اثنان اثنان ثم خرجوا به ودفنو خارج الدير ونحن ما خرحنا معه ما هي عاد وان كان المايت غني دا بياخدوا جميع الحاضرين الي بيته الي المايده ثم بيانوا بصحن سليقه وبيفرقوا عليهم شمع والكاهن افلوجيطوس ثم طروباربات الموتي والشماس بعد البخور ارحمنا يا الله بتمامها وبيدكر المايت ورشيس الكبنه الافشين وبيتناولوا! من السليقه وبيترحموا عليه ا
then three other genuflections and mounted to his throne. Then the bishop and the hegumen of the convent came each from a different side. They made genuflections toward the east and they kissed the icon. The priests also came as well as the deacons and the monks who advanced two by two. Two of the relatives or friends of the dead stood on both sides, distributing to the whole assembly an alms intended for the poor, wrapped in sheets of paper or in embroidered handkerchiefs. Then the notables entered according to their rank, two by two, after the crowd; then the deceased was transported out of the church and he was buried outside the convent, but we did not accompany the body, for it is not the custom. When the dead man is very rich, all the assistants are invited to a meal in the house of mourning. A tray of colyva is brought and candles are distributed and the priest says: "Blessed" and the troparia of the dead. After having incensed, the deacon says: "Have mercy on us, O God" etc. ... then he mentions the name of the dead. The archpriest says the prayer, one takes of the colyva and one prays for the deceased.
اخبار الحوادث الذي صارت واما يوم الاحد الخامس من الصوم صلينا في كنيسه الدير وكان حضرةٍ البيك اوعد
XIX. - Chronicle of past events. On the fifth Sunday of Lent, we performed prayers in the church of the convent and His Highness the prince made my lord the patriarch promise


[p. 474] {#po.24.p474}
سيدنا الطرك ان يقدس في دير هارون ونوضه خارج البلد ولكنه اصبح في هذا الوم مفتاض كثير لا تعلم لماذا ٌ
to celebrate Mass in the convent of the Voivode Aron, outside the city.
يجب ان تعلم ان الساعة الحديدية التي في دير الافقدي كاات تدق من اول ادار الى عشرة منه انني عش ساعه لاا وانني عشر ساعة ليلا سوا ومن بعد العشرلا ايام اندارت تتدق اربعة عش ساعة نهار وعشرة ساعات لِلّا
But on the morning of this day, he was very angry, and we were ignorant of the cause. It is necessary to know that the iron clock of the princely convent rang from the first of March until the tenth of this month, twelve hours during the night and twelve hours during the day, and for the following ten days, it rang fourteen hours during the day and ten hours during the night.


[p. 475] {#po.24.p475}
واما جري! بعد هذد الحوادث انه كان سبب قلة خروج البيك فى هذا اليوم الي برا هو هذا وهو ان اتاد ورقه مع كاهن معلم اعتراف ان اكاب بلاده قد خانوا عليه واتفقوا مع ماغس لوغوتاتيس على قتله ومضي داك من مد عشرةٍ ايام بحيله الى عند قرال المج اي الاونكروس والي عند متتي بيك الفلاخ ملت اليهم وشنع علي فاسلي ان مرادة يملك بسيفه وقوة القزق بلاد المج وبلاد الفلاخ فاغتاضوا عليه سبب ذلك كثيراً او اعطوا لماغس لوغاتي3 المذكور قوة عظيمه منهم نحو ثلاثون الف عكري لياتوا على فاسيلي وقتله وكان قد سجد ليم واتفق هو واياهم ان يكونوا شي واحد وصار الميعاد بين ماغس لوغاتى5 المذكور وبين أكابر عساكر الغضان انها يكبسوا فاسيلى على غفله يوم الاحد الشعائين حين القداس فى ديرا لان عادته كات انه يمل عيد الشمانين فيه ديم بالافراح والسرور ثم يحتووا علي ساير حزاته وقتلود فابادي ما تح
After these events, a fact occurred which prevented the prince from going out that day. A letter had been sent to him by a confessor priest informing him of the treason of the boyars of the country; these had conspired with the grand "logothete" to kill him. The latter, using ruse, had gone ten days earlier to the King of the Magyars * or Hungarians and to Matthew, prince of Wallachia, to take refuge with them. He imputed to Basil the desire to conquer by the sword and with the support of the Cossacks, the land of the Magyars and Wallachia. They became irritated against Prince Basil and provided the grand logothete with a large army of thirty thousand soldiers, to march against Basil and kill him. The grand logothete had prostrated himself before them and he had come to an understanding with them to act in common accord. It had been agreed between the grand logothete and the commanders of the armies of Moldavia to surprise Basil, unexpectedly, on Palm Sunday, at the moment when Mass was being celebrated in his convent; because he had the habit of celebrating Palm Sunday there with joy, they wanted then to seize his treasures and kill him. But the Creator did not permit it; and He made the truth known to him by


[p. 476] {#po.24.p476}
بذلك واظهر له في الورقه المذكوره ذلك لان خيراته واحساناته في كل العالم دفعت عنه فلما سمع بذلك بالحال عزل صرايته وقلع حريته وجميع امواله وارزاقه الي القلعه المسملا خوتيني وهي علي حافه سنار اللاه وجميع عساكره ولكن كانوا جميعهم خايئين عليه فمضي حينيد الي القلعه المذكورة ومنها يوم سبت النور قطع النهر ودخل بلاد اللاه الي القلعه المسادا كامانيتصا وارسل مكاتيب للقسطنطينيه! يعلمهم بما جري ثم ارسل ستنجد اخميل خثمان القزق وابنه صهرلا ثيموتاوس الجديد ثم واستنجد الخان والتطر وقتّل قبل ما يخرج من .ياش ثلثه انفار من اكابر عساكر الواحد كان سردار العسكر واثنان غيل وكان فاسيلى بيك قد صار اشبينه والضومنا اشبيئته وكان يحه للغاية غير خاي عنه اسرارنة لا انه هو كان مباطنا عليه فأرسل مرا الى عند قرال المجر راكوتس ايلج فراح رط معه علي قلع باسيل من كرينيه زاعمً انه ارسل للترك ودفع ليم مالا لبحط اخيه قرلا في المج وحقتى ذلك عنده وكذلك أرسله ايلج لعند متتي وووضه الفلاخ وهكذا كذب
the letter in question, because the acts of charity and the alms he did throughout the whole world had protected him. He immediately left his palace and sent his wife and all his goods with provisions to the fortress of Hotin on the border of Poland, as well as all his troops, but all betrayed him. He then left for this fortress on Holy Saturday; from there he crossed the river and entered Poland into the fortress named Kamenetz. He sent letters to Constantinople to make known what had happened, then he asked for help from Hmilnitzki, hetman of the Cossacks, and from his new son-in-law Timothy, son of the hetman; he also asked for the help of the Khan and the Tatars. Before leaving Jassy, he killed three people among the commanders of his army: the first was the grand sirdar of the troops and two others. Prince Basil had been godfather [to the grand logothete] and the princess his godmother; he loved him very much and had no secrets from him, for he was intimate with him. Once he sent him to Rakoczy, King of the Hungarians, in the capacity of ambassador; he went there and conspired with him to dethrone Basil, claiming that he had sent an agent to the Turks to offer them money in order to designate his brother as King of the Hungarians, and he made him believe it. Likewise, he had sent him on an embassy to Voivode Matthew of Wallachia, and the hetman lied to him in the same way, saying that he had proposed money to the Turks to obtain the throne of Wallachia for his son, Voivode Stephen. The other


[p. 477] {#po.24.p477}
عليه انه ارسل للترك يدقع لهم مالا لياخذ كرسي الفلا لابنه ستافايصا ايض فصدقه الآخر وتراسلوا مع قرال المج وريطوا رياط مع اللوغاتي المذكور على انه هو يصير بك علي البغضان بقوتهم وهم ينجدونه بساكرهم فلا زال يراسل وطبخ ويجتهد حتي اطاعته اكابر البغضان لبغضتهم العظيمه لباسيل واكاب» وجنسه الروم خصوصً لان كان له ابن اخ اسمه!... افد اربعه الاف نت وكانوا اذا اشتكوا اهاليهم عليه لا رشت عليه حق لانه كان يكيس البيوت بالليالي وبخظف النات وغير ذلك من الظلم ولا زال يتحيل لمدة غائه سنوات سر الي ان ارسل طبخ لحاله في اسطنبول3 ايض وكانوا الترك نون ذلك ثم ان فاسيلي استدعاة واعطاد عشرد الى دينار ليخفبها فعمل حيله وأرسل امراته الي قراة في بغضانا وعد كم يوم اخرج له مكتوب بانها ضعيفه وهي علي حال التكد فاعطلا اجازه بالمضى اليبا وهذا بعد ان كان قد ضحك واصرف عنه اولايك السكمان النمارده الذي كان سالج بهم من مدة سنين من الروم والسرب وللبلغار وغيرهم بحيلة قابلًا له لنت ما عليك عدو فلما تصرف علييم هذ النفقات
believed it too. The Wallachians entered into correspondence with the King of the Hungarians. They conspired with the aforementioned "logothete" so that he would become Prince of Moldavia thanks to their support and with the help of their army. He did not cease to correspond and intrigue until he had won over the boyars of Moldavia. Thanks to the violent hatred they felt for Basil, for his boyars, and for his Greek nation above all, and because he had a nephew, named ... who had corrupted four thousand girls, and when their parents complained about him, it was impossible to prove his guilt, because he would enter houses unexpectedly during the night, abduct the girls by force, etc. For three years, he used only secret ruses to achieve his goal through intrigues in Constantinople itself, and the Turks appreciated this. Then Basil called him and gave him ten thousand dinars that he was to put in a safe place. He used ruses and sent his wife to a village in Moldavia: after that, one day, he showed him a letter in which she was said to be sick, but it was a pretext. Then the prince granted him permission to go to her. After having thus mocked the prince, he had dismissed * the famous "seimens," valiant soldiers who had been in the prince's service for years, and whose corps was composed of Greeks, Serbs, Bulgarians, etc. To get rid of them, he had used ruses, telling him: "You have no enemies, why go to all this expense."


[p. 478] {#po.24.p478}
وكان كل سحر سبت من الصوم يصرف منهم عدة بيارق الي ان أصرفيم وتمت له الحيله لان عسكر اللاد كان خاين عليه! وكان خروج فاسيل" من باش يوم الخميس جمعة الشعائين ولو لا انه يرسل ابن اخيه بساك وقطع3 جسر عظيم في طريق عدوه ولا كان كبسه في البلد لانه اتعوق يؤمين حتى اعاد الجسر وجا فما لحقه وكنت تنظر يا هذا الخوف والفزع الذي حدث بعد خروج السبع من البلد لان اهالي اللاد جميعهم صاروا حراميه جلليه واجتمعت جميع البازركان واهل البلد وحابوا جميع ارزاقهم الي الديورة وتحصنوا ها وعملوا حول كل دير متاديس العرابات وكان خوف وفزع عظيم عليهم وعلينا لاننا بعد في دهرنا ما اصرنا شي من هذا وحينيد؟ بطات الصلوات والقداسات لان الكناين والبياكل امتلت الي السقوف من الحوايج والارذاق
Every Saturday morning of Lent, he dismissed a certain number of "seimens" banners until he had discharged them all. His ruse succeeded completely, for the country's army betrayed the prince. Basil left Jassy on the Thursday of Palm Week, and if he had not sent his nephew with his army to destroy a large bridge that was on the path of his enemies, they would have surprised him in the city; indeed, they were stopped for two days to rebuild the bridge; he left, but they did not reach him. Immediately after the departure of the lion (that is to say, the prince), fear and terror seized the population of the city, for the inhabitants of the country all became rebellious thieves. All the merchants and the population of the city gathered and transported their goods into the convents; they entrenched themselves there, making barricades around each convent by means of carts. A great terror reigned among them, and we shared it, for in our lives we had not witnessed such events. Prayers and masses ceased because the churches and sanctuaries were packed with merchandise and provisions piled up to the vaults.


[p. 479] {#po.24.p479}
